
=== 1. an extrusion needs a sketch
  document: {"parameters":[],"checks":[],"settings":{"chordTolerance":0.01},"schemaVersion":10}
  toolbar:
      div.group
        button title="Empty document": "New"
        button: "Open"
        button: "Save"
        button disabled: "Export STL"
        input type=file hidden
      div.group
        button title="Connect to the MCP server (packages/mcp): the AI then reads and changes the document open in the app": "AI"
      div.group
        button disabled title="Ctrl+Z": "↶ Undo"
        button disabled title="Ctrl+Shift+Z": "↷ Redo"
      div.group
        button title="A new sketch on a plane or a planar face": "Sketch"
        button title="Extrude regions of a sketch": "Extrude"
        button title="Turn regions of a sketch about an axis in its plane": "Revolve"
        button title="Holes for screws, nuts and inserts at points of a sketch": "Hole"
        button title="Chamfer edges of a body by a distance": "Chamfer"
        button title="Round edges of a body by a radius": "Fillet"
        button title="A plane offset from a plane or a planar face": "Plane"
        button title="Repeat extrusions that add or cut, or a body: around an axis, along a direction, or mirrored in a plane": "Pattern"
        button title="Place a stored mesh (a bought part read from an STL file) as a body": "Mesh"
        button title="Insert another document (a part saved as a document file) as a part of this one": "Insert"
        button title="Join, subtract or intersect finished bodies; subtract with a clearance": "Combine"
        button title="Cut a body into two by a plane; both parts get the cut face": "Split"
      div.group
        select value="" title="Ask a query about the model"
          option selected value="": "Queries…"
          option value="interference" title="Which bodies share a volume, pair by pair. Bodies that only touch are not reported.": "Interference"
          option value="sketchDiagnosis" title="How far a sketch is determined: its degrees of freedom, the entities that can still move, and the constraints that are duplicate, redundant or in conflict.": "Diagnosis of a sketch"
          option value="constraintExplanation" title="Whether a constraint of a sketch follows from other constraints, and from which.": "Explanation of a constraint"
          option value="sketchContacts" title="Where the curves of a sketch that form regions cross, touch, end on each other or overlap apart from common points, which is what makes its profile invalid, and what the sketch tool split would do at each place. Ask it when a sketch reports `valid: false`, before split and trim.": "Contacts of the outline curves of a sketch"
          option value="sketchSources" title="The sketches before a sketch on the timeline and whether each lies on a parallel plane, and, for one of them, every point and curve a projection may name, placed in the coordinates of the asking sketch. Ask it before addExternal with `type: "sketch"` or the sketch tool projectSketch.": "What a sketch can project from earlier sketches"
          option value="body" title="A body in detail: its faces with their names, surfaces and areas, and its edges with their names and shapes. The names are what references use: the plane of a sketch on a face, the projection of an edge, an entity in the arguments of another query.": "Description of a body"
          option value="catalog" title="A table of data the engine carries, such as sizes of hardware or densities of materials. A catalog fills in fields; the document stores the numbers, not a reference to the row.": "Data catalog"
          option value="measure" title="Distance and angle between two faces, edges or sketch points. Exact for planes, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori, straight and circular edges and sketch points; the smallest distance on the mesh for every pair. The time grows with the number of triangles of the two faces.": "Measure"
          option value="section" title="What a plane cuts out of the bodies: closed loops with their areas and, for every piece of a loop, the face it lies on.": "Section"
          option value="gap" title="How close bodies are to each other: for every pair within the search distance the smallest distance between their surfaces and where it is. Two bodies ask about one pair. Bodies that touch or collide have the distance zero. Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
          option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`). A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`, and so is a reference part without `bodies` when it states neither a mass nor a density; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
          option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
          option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
          option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
          option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
          option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
          option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
        select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
          option selected value="": "Export…"
          option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
          option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
          option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
          option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
          option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
          option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
          option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
        button disabled title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"
      div.group
        button: "Fit all"
        button disabled title="Save a picture of the model as PNG: the bodies that are shown, from the camera of the view and cut as the view is; without the selection, sketches, planes and dimensions": "Save picture"
        button.active title="Show or hide the planes XY, XZ, YZ": "Base planes"
  timeline:
      h2: "Timeline"
      p.hint: "Empty so far. New features will appear here."
  panel:
      h2: "Model"
      p.hint: "The right mouse button orbits, the wheel zooms, the middle button (or Shift + right) pans. On a touchpad: Alt/Option + drag orbits, Alt + Shift + drag pans, two fingers zoom. A feature on the timeline is edited by a double click."
      h2: "Parameters (0)"
      div.constraint
        input.pname value="" placeholder="name"
        input.expr value="" placeholder="expression, e.g. 2 * a"
        button: "Add"
      p.hint: "An expression can be typed into numeric fields and dimensions instead of a number. Angles in an expression are in radians; write degrees as deg(30)."
      h2: "Checks (0)"
      div.constraint
        input.pname value="" placeholder="name"
        input.expr value="" placeholder="condition, e.g. a < b"
        button: "Add"
  status: An extrude needs a sketch. Draw one first.  [error]
  view: 1 scene(s) set
  scene:
      bodies: (none)
      line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 509.223
      line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 339.482
      line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 169.741
      fill #8b9bb4 opacity 0.08 at [0, 0, 0] normal [0, 0, 1]: 8 numbers, sum 24.249
      fill #8b9bb4 opacity 0.08 at [0, 0, 0] normal [0, -1, 0]: 8 numbers, sum 24.249
      fill #8b9bb4 opacity 0.08 at [0, 0, 0] normal [1, 0, 0]: 8 numbers, sum 24.249
  view: fit [-20, -20, -20] .. [20, 20, 20]

=== 2. the base sketch: two regions
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addFeature","feature":{"id":"s1","name":"Sketch 1","suppressed":false,"type":"sketch","plane":{"kind":"base","plane":"XY"},"points":{},"radii":{},"curves":{},"constraints":{},"external":{},"solved":{"inputs":"","external":{}}},"index":0}]}) -> ok
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x5)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p1","x":40,"y":0},{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p2","x":40,"y":30},{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p3","x":0,"y":30},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c1","curve":{"type":"line","a":"origin","b":"p1"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k1","constraint":{"type":"horizontal","line":"c1"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c2","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p1","b":"p2"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k2","constraint":{"type":"vertical","line":"c2"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c3","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p2","b":"p3"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k3","constraint":{"type":"horizontal","line":"c3"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c4","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p3","b":"origin"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k4","constraint":{"type":"vertical","line":"c4"}}]}) -> ok
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x5)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p4","x":70,"y":15},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c5","curve":{"type":"circle","center":"p4"},"radius":8}]}) -> ok
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x3)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p5","x":20,"y":15}]}) -> ok
  features: s1
  feature s1:
      {"id":"s1","name":"Sketch 1","suppressed":false,"type":"sketch","plane":{"kind":"base","plane":"XY"}}
      point p1: {"x":40,"y":0}
      point p2: {"x":40,"y":30}
      point p3: {"x":0,"y":30}
      point p4: {"x":70,"y":15}
      point p5: {"x":20,"y":15}
      radius c5: 8
      curve c1: {"type":"line","a":"origin","b":"p1"}
      curve c2: {"type":"line","a":"p1","b":"p2"}
      curve c3: {"type":"line","a":"p2","b":"p3"}
      curve c4: {"type":"line","a":"p3","b":"origin"}
      curve c5: {"type":"circle","center":"p4"}
      constraint k1: {"type":"horizontal","line":"c1"}
      constraint k2: {"type":"vertical","line":"c2"}
      constraint k3: {"type":"horizontal","line":"c3"}
      constraint k4: {"type":"vertical","line":"c4"}
  toolbar:
    +   button title="Ctrl+Z": "↶ Undo"
    -   button disabled title="Ctrl+Z": "↶ Undo"
  timeline:
    + div.row.ok.selected
    +   span.dot: "●"
    +   span.name: "Sketch 1"
    +   button.icon title="Hide or show": "👁"

    +   button.icon disabled title="Move later": "↓"
    +   button.icon title="Roll the model back to here: further features are inserted after this one": "⇤"
    - p.hint: "Empty so far. New features will appear here."
  panel:
    + h2: "Sketch"
    + label
    +   "Feature name"
    +   input value="Sketch 1"
    + div.buttons
    +   button.primary: "Edit feature"
    +   button: "Suppress"
    +   button: "Delete"
    + button disabled title="First select a plane or a planar face in the view": "Move the sketch to the selected plane"
    - h2: "Model"
    - p.hint: "The right mouse button orbits, the wheel zooms, the middle button (or Shift + right) pans. On a touchpad: Alt/Option + drag orbits, Alt + Shift + drag pans, two fingers zoom. A feature on the timeline is edited by a double click."
  status: Sketch finished.
  view: 19 scene(s) set
  scene:
    + line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 559.759
    + line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 373.173
    + line #8b9bb4 width 1: 24 numbers, sum 186.586
    + line #f97316 width 2.5: 600 numbers, sum 66807.628
    + fill #8b9bb4 opacity 0.08 at [0, 0, 0] normal [0, 0, 1]: 8 numbers, sum 26.655
    + fill #8b9bb4 opacity 0.08 at [0, 0, 0] normal [0, -1, 0]: 8 numbers, sum 26.655

  view: lookAt origin [0, 0, 0] normal [0, 0, 1]

  view: mark at [0, 0, 0]
  view: mark at [40, 30, 0]
  view: mark at [70, 15, 0]
  view: mark at [78, 15, 0]
  view: mark at [20, 15, 0]

=== 3. a new extrusion: two regions, none picked, nothing to compute or confirm

    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
    +     option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    +     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    +     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
    +   button disabled title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"
    + div.group
    +   button title="Save a picture of the model as PNG: the bodies that are shown, from the camera of the view and cut as the view is; without the selection, sketches, planes and dimensions": "Save picture"

    +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`).     +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`, and so is a reference part without `bodies` when it states neither a mass nor a density; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    -     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    -     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
    -     option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
    -   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    -     option selected value="": "Export…"
    -     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    -     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    -     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    -     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    -     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    -     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    -     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"

The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
    +     option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    +     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    +     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
    +   button title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"

    -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`). A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`, and so is a reference part without `bodies` when it states neither a mass nor a density; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"

    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none.     +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    -     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
    -     option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
    -   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    -     option selected value="": "Export…"
    -     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    -     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    -     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    -     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    -     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    -     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    -     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
    -   button title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"

A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern. A spline comes with its control points (`control`: `<id>/<i>` stands at `control[i]`), which constraints and projections may name, and where it runs.": "The curves of an entry of a sketch"
    +     option value="sketchRegions" title="The regions of a sketch with their whole references, as an extrusion or a revolution names them (`{ outerLoop: [...] }`), a page at a time: get_sketch prints a loop of more than twenty curves short, with the `index` to ask here. `curve` keeps the regions a curve bounds (the way from a curve of an imported outline to the region to extrude); `holes` lists the curves of the holes too.": "The regions of a sketch"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    +     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    +     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
    +   button title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"

    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted).     +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`).     +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`). A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`, and so is a reference part without `bodies` when it states neither a mass nor a density; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    -     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error.     -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    -     option value="entryCurves" title="The curves of one entry of `external` of a sketch at their places, a page at a time: of an imported outline (get_sketch lists its first curves only), of a projection, or the copies of a mirror or pattern.     +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
    +     option value="urdf" title="The mechanism as a URDF robot description for ROS and simulators: a folder with robot.urdf and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a visual and a collision mesh per body. Every revolute and prismatic joint needs `effort` and `velocity` (setJoint). The root is not fixed: the simulator decides.": "URDF (robot)"
    +     option value="mjcf" title="The mechanism as a MuJoCo model: a folder with robot.xml and one binary STL per body in metres in the frame of its link. One tree from one root link, welded to the world; the frame of a link at the axis of its joint with the axes of the model; the mass, centre of mass and inertia of each link from its bodies (query mass); a mesh geom per body, the two links of a joint excluded from contact; a motor per joint with an `effort` (with `gearRatio`), `rotorInertia · gearRatio²` as the armature of the joint, and the pose of the document as the keyframe `pose`.": "MJCF (MuJoCo)"
    +   button title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"

    -     option value="catalog" title="A table of data the engine carries, such as sizes of hardware or densities of materials. A catalog fills in fields; the document stores the numbers, not a reference to the row.": "Data catalog"

Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece (all bodies of the state without `bodies`). A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`, and so is a reference part without `bodies` when it states neither a mass nor a density; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    -     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error.     -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
